I had this whole routine. I'd stop at this little coffee shop near the office—not one of the big chains, a local place called \"The Daily Grind.\" Kind of a cliché name, I know. But they had this amazing cold brew. They steeped it for like 24 hours, so it was super smooth, not bitter at all. And the barista, her name was Chloe, she always remembered my order. She had this cool streak of blue in her hair that she'd change up sometimes. One week it was purple, another week it was pink. I asked her about it once, and she said she used this special vegan dye that washes out after a few weeks so she doesn't ruin her hair. I don't know much about hair dye, but it seemed like a lot of upkeep. I can barely remember to get a haircut every six weeks. My barber, Tony, he's always giving me a hard time about it. He's this old Italian guy who's been cutting hair in the same spot since the eighties. He still uses those warm shaving cream dispensers for the back of your neck. It feels incredible, but he talks your ear off about local politics the whole time. \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being down. No, I've never had anything drag on for weeks like this before. It's totally new for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than just a few days, even if it wasn't as bad as what you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it hasn't ever been this bad before, but... yeah, lately I just don't care about anything I used to like doing. I used to play soccer on the weekends, cook dinner for my girlfriend, go check out these small art galleries downtown... I'd even stick around the office to help out the newer sales guys. But now I just can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nWhich is a shame about the cooking, really. My girlfriend loved when I'd make my famous chicken parmesan. The secret is to double-bread the chicken, you know? Flour, egg, breadcrumbs, then back in the egg and breadcrumbs again. It makes this crazy thick crust that holds up to the sauce. I use San Marzano tomatoes for the sauce—they're expensive, like five bucks a can, but totally worth it. The regular ones just taste like tin. I actually went to this Italian grocery store across town to get them, and the parking lot there is a nightmare. The spaces are so narrow, I swear they were painted for golf carts. You couldn't even see his lips move when he talked, so half the time I didn't even know he was giving me a direction until he started yelling. Okay, doc, I'm... I'm really starting to wonder if I'm speaking a different language here. Or maybe the acoustics in this room are just really bad? I know sometimes the soundproofing in these medical buildings can do weird things. Like, I was at the dentist last month—Dr. Gable, great guy, terrible taste in music though, always plays smooth jazz—and I could hear the drill from three rooms over like it was right next to my ear. I took it to the dealership and the guy tried to tell me it was my phone, but I just bought this phone three months ago. Anyway, I ended up driving in silence for like two hours yesterday. But yeah, I'll just totally forget to eat all day while I'm out there, and then other nights I'm just tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the times when you're eating a lot of takeout and snacks after midnight? Do you feel like you're losing control during those episodes or eating much more than most people would in a similar period of time?\n\nPatient: I mean, no, I don't think I'm like... losing control or anything. It's not a crazy amount of food, just a normal meal and maybe some chips. It's usually just because I'm finally sitting down for the day. Like, I'll order from this Thai place down the street—they make this really good Pad Kee Mao, but they always, *always* mess up the spice level. I ask for medium, and it's either totally bland or it absolutely burns my face off. \n\nAnd the worst part is the delivery guys can never find my building. My apartment complex has the weirdest numbering system. Building 4 is right next to Building 12, and then Building 5 is on the complete opposite side of the property by the dumpsters. I don't know who designed it, probably some architect who just threw darts at a board. So half the time I'm out in the parking lot at 1 AM in my sweatpants, waving my phone flashlight around just trying to flag down my noodles. But yeah, I don't think I'm eating way more than anyone else would. Just eating late, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, hopeless, or losing interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean... I guess I have been losing interest in stuff. I used to be pretty active. Like, I'd play soccer on the weekends, I'd cook dinner for my girlfriend, we'd go check out these small art galleries downtown, and at work I'd always take time to help the newer sales guys learn the ropes. But lately... I don't know, I just can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nWhich is honestly pretty rough on the new guys, because the company just hired this kid, Jason. Nice guy, but he wears these ridiculously loud ties, like neon geometric patterns, I don't even know where you buy them. Anyway, he accidentally ordered three thousand units of the wrong ball bearings last week. Three thousand! And our warehouse manager, Don—who is this old-school guy who practically lives on black coffee and spite—he just lost his absolute mind. You could hear him yelling all the way out by the loading dock. I usually try to step in and save the new kids before Don eats them alive, but man, Don's blood pressure must be insane. He drives this beat-up old Ford truck that squeaks every time he turns the wheel, and I keep telling him I can get him a discount on the suspension parts, but he never listens to me. \n\nBut yeah, what were we talking about? Oh, right. Yeah, I just haven't had the energy to care about my usual stuff lately.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the loss of interest and not caring about your usual activities?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months, I think. Yeah, four months of just this really low mood, feeling guilty about everything, and just... not caring. It definitely got harder to ignore after some trouble I had at work. \n\nBut honestly, work has been a nightmare lately anyway. Like, they completely changed our expense reporting software last month. It used to be this super simple spreadsheet, right? You just plug in your mileage, your meals, whatever. Now it's this cloud-based app that requires two-factor authentication every single time you open it. And half the time, the authentication text takes like twenty minutes to come through, so by the time I get the code, the app has timed out and I have to start all over. I spent three hours last Sunday just trying to submit my gas receipts. And the receipts! The gas station by my house prints them on this weird thermal paper that fades if you even look at it wrong, so I have to take a picture immediately or it just looks like a blank white square by the time I get home. I swear, corporate just sits around trying to find new ways to make our lives miserable. But yeah, it's been about four months of feeling this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep—like trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty messed up. Most nights I either can't fall asleep because my mind just keeps running through all my sales pitches, or I wake up way before my alarm and feel completely wired, even though I know I barely slept at all. \n\nWhich is honestly the worst feeling, especially when I have to be on the road the next day. I drive this 2018 Honda Civic, right? Good car, reliable, gets decent gas mileage. But the driver's side seat has this weird lump right in the lower lumbar area. I don't know if the foam just compressed weirdly or what, but after about two hours of driving, my back is just screaming. I tried buying one of those beaded seat covers, you know, the ones taxi drivers always have? I figured they must know something I don't. But all it did was pinch the back of my legs when I wore shorts. I ended up giving it to my buddy Mark. Mark's a funny guy, he actually collects vintage bowling shirts. He has like, fifty of them in his closet. He wears them to our company barbecues and always insists on grilling the hot dogs, even though he burns them every single time. \n\nBut yeah, sleep. It's just really hard to turn my brain off, or I wake up super early and can't go back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt this way before, with a loss of interest and low mood, or is this the first time you've experienced these kinds of feelings?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've had bad days, sure, but nothing like this where it just drags on and on. I've always been pretty upbeat, you know? Like, I was the guy organizing the office fantasy football league. \n\nWhich, by the way, is a completely thankless job. You spend all this time setting up the draft, making sure everyone pays their buy-in, and then half the guys don't even set their lineups by week three. Last year, Sarah from accounting won the whole thing, and I'm pretty sure she just auto-drafted and then forgot about it. She didn't even know who her quarterback was when I handed her the prize money. She used it to buy this enormous, terrifyingly realistic ceramic owl for her desk. She says it's supposed to scare away mice, but we're on the fourth floor of an office building. I don't think we have a mouse problem. The only pest we have is the guy who keeps microwaving fish in the breakroom on Tuesdays. It smells like a pier at low tide for the rest of the afternoon. I actually bought a little can of air freshener to keep in my desk just because of him. It's supposed to smell like \"mountain spring,\" but it really just smells like cheap laundry detergent and regret. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, this is the first time I've felt this... this low for this long.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently—either gaining or losing more than usual?\n\nPatient: Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place. Like, some days I just completely forget meals, and then other nights I'm tearing through takeout and snacks after midnight. \n\nWhich is terrible for my budget, by the way. Have you seen the delivery fees on those apps lately? It's out of control. I ordered a burger the other night, just a regular cheeseburger and fries, and by the time they added the service fee, the delivery fee, and the tip, it was like thirty bucks. And the fries weren't even crispy! They put them in those styrofoam containers, and the steam just makes everything soggy. I tried putting them in the air fryer to crisp them back up—I bought this ninja air fryer on Black Friday last year, it was like fifty percent off, great deal. It has two baskets so you can cook two different things at once. I usually do chicken wings on one side and Brussels sprouts on the other. You toss the sprouts in a little balsamic glaze and bacon bits, they come out amazing. But it doesn't work on soggy delivery fries, they just turn into little grease sponges. I should probably just delete the app, but then I'm stuck trying to cook at 1 AM when I realize I haven't eaten all day. But yeah, my eating habits and my weight have just been totally erratic lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any previous episodes in your life where you felt down, lost interest in things, or had trouble functioning for more than a couple of weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying before, this is really the first time it's been this bad. But if I think about it, this whole... whatever it is, this low mood and feeling guilty all the time and just not caring about my accounts or anything... it's been hanging over me for about four months now. It definitely got a lot harder to ignore after some trouble I had at work. \n\nWhich, man, the wholesale business is just cutthroat right now. Everyone's trying to undercut everyone else. We had this one client, a big regional hardware chain, and I had been buttering up their purchasing manager for like six months. Taking him to lunches, sending him those ridiculous gourmet popcorn tins around the holidays—you know the ones, with the three flavors divided by cardboard? Caramel, cheese, and plain. I don't know who actually eats the plain section, it's basically just packing peanuts. Anyway, I thought I had the account locked down, and then out of nowhere, this guy from a rival firm swoops in and steals it. And get this, the guy who stole it? He used to play minor league baseball. He brings it up in every conversation. \"Oh, that reminds me of when I was playing double-A ball in Toledo.\" Like, nobody cares, Brad! You hit .210 and washed out, you're not Derek Jeter. He even wears his old team windbreaker to trade shows. It's so pathetic. \n\nBut yeah, after that whole mess with the hardware account, that's when this feeling really settled in and I just couldn't shake it anymore. It's been about four months of just... feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks in the past where you felt down or lost interest in things, even if it wasn't as bad as this time?\n\nPatient: No, really, like I said, nothing lasting two weeks or anything like that before this recent stretch. I mean, sure, I've had a bad weekend here and there, especially if my team loses or something, but I always bounced back by Monday. \n\nI actually used to look forward to Mondays, believe it or not. I had this whole routine. I'd stop at this little coffee shop near the office—not one of the big chains, a local place called \"The Daily Grind.\" Kind of a cliché name, I know. But they had this amazing cold brew. They steeped it for like 24 hours, so it was super smooth, not bitter at all. And the barista, her name was Chloe, she always remembered my order. She had this cool streak of blue in her hair that she'd change up sometimes. One week it was purple, another week it was pink. I asked her about it once, and she said she used this special vegan dye that washes out after a few weeks so she doesn't ruin her hair. I don't know much about hair dye, but it seemed like a lot of upkeep. I can barely remember to get a haircut every six weeks. My barber, Tony, he's always giving me a hard time about it. He's this old Italian guy who's been cutting hair in the same spot since the eighties. He still uses those warm shaving cream dispensers for the back of your neck. It feels incredible, but he talks your ear off about local politics the whole time. \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being down. No, I've never had anything drag on for weeks like this before. It's totally new for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than just a few days, even if it wasn't as bad as what you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it hasn't ever been this bad before, but... yeah, lately I just don't care about anything I used to like doing. I used to play soccer on the weekends, cook dinner for my girlfriend, go check out these small art galleries downtown... I'd even stick around the office to help out the newer sales guys. But now I just can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nWhich is a shame about the cooking, really. My girlfriend loved when I'd make my famous chicken parmesan. The secret is to double-bread the chicken, you know? Flour, egg, breadcrumbs, then back in the egg and breadcrumbs again. It makes this crazy thick crust that holds up to the sauce. I use San Marzano tomatoes for the sauce—they're expensive, like five bucks a can, but totally worth it. The regular ones just taste like tin. I actually went to this Italian grocery store across town to get them, and the parking lot there is a nightmare. The spaces are so narrow, I swear they were painted for golf carts. I saw this lady in a massive SUV try to wedge into a spot and she totally scraped the side of a parked Honda. She just drove off! I couldn't believe it. I tried to get her license plate, but she sped out of there so fast. I ended up leaving a note on the Honda's windshield, just saying what happened. I hope they saw it before it rained, because we had that huge thunderstorm later that afternoon. The thunder was so loud it shook my apartment windows. My neighbor's dog wouldn't stop barking for like three hours. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I haven't cooked or played soccer or done any of that stuff in months. I just don't have the interest anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than just a few days?\n\nPatient: I feel like we keep circling back to this, doc, but no, I really haven't. The instructor had this massive walrus mustache that entirely covered his mouth. You couldn't even see his lips move when he talked, so half the time I didn't even know he was giving me a direction until he started yelling. And he made me parallel park on this massive hill over on 4th street. Have you ever tried parking a 1998 Ford Taurus on a steep incline? The car was an absolute boat. My dad bought it at a police auction for like eight hundred bucks. It still had the spotlight attached to the driver's side door, which I thought was the coolest thing in the world at the time, but the suspension was completely shot. Every time you hit a pothole, the whole chassis would just rattle. \n\nI actually ended up selling it to this guy on Craigslist a few years later who said he was going to enter it in a demolition derby at the county fair. I always wondered how it did. Probably got crushed in the first round, honestly, the frame was half rust by the time I handed over the keys. The guy paid me entirely in twenty dollar bills, which was kind of sketchy, but hey, cash is cash, right? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'd be upset about stuff like the driver's test for a couple days, but never anything that just dragged on and on for weeks like what's happening to me now. This is completely new territory.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this current one that lasted more than a few days where you felt down or lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: Doc, are we stuck in a time loop or something? And every single time I call him to reorder, he asks me if the price on the bulk degreaser went up. Every time. I always tell him Gary, it is the exact same price it was six months ago. He is just convinced we are sneaking in hidden fees because our invoice paper changed. We switched to this recycled paper at the office to be more eco-friendly, you know? It has those little brown flecks in it. Honestly, it jams the printer constantly. I spent like forty minutes last Tuesday just pulling shredded bits of recycled paper out of the roller. \n\nMy manager was hovering over me the whole time, breathing through his nose really loudly. He does that when he gets stressed out. He has this terrible deviated septum from breaking his nose playing rugby in college. I guess I drink a lot of milk. \n\nAnyway, no, I have never had any long stretches of feeling down before this current one. I really do not know how else to tell you.\n\nDoctor: Between this current episode and any earlier periods when you felt down or lost interest in things, even if brief, have you had at least two months where your mood was completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, I've never really had those earlier periods of feeling down, so... I guess I've just been normal my whole life until now? Yeah, completely normal mood. Just regular. \n\nExcept for when I'm at work, I guess. The vents are right above my desk and they always blow this weird, dusty air directly onto my neck. I asked maintenance to look at it, and this guy came up with a ladder that looked like it was from the 1950s. It was wooden and had all these paint splatters on it. He didn't even fix the vent, he just taped a piece of cardboard over half of it. It looks so unprofessional. But I guess it keeps the dust off me. \n\nBut yeah, I haven't had any of those down periods before this, so I've just been my regular self.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, have you ever had a time when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than a couple of weeks, followed by a period of at least two months where you felt completely back to normal?\n\nPatient: Doc, seriously, are you messing with me right now? But they wanted like a hundred bucks a year for it. For a magazine! And it's mostly just full-page ads for forklifts and pallet jacks anyway. \n\nI actually got to drive a forklift once at our main warehouse. It is way harder than it looks. The steering is in the back wheels, so when you turn, the whole back end just swings out. I almost knocked over an entire pallet of industrial antifreeze. The warehouse manager, this guy named Dave who always wears these bright orange suspenders, he yelled at me for like ten minutes straight. His face got so red I thought he was going to have a heart attack right there on the concrete. \n\nBut anyway... yeah. To answer your question for the fourth time, no. Never happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time in your life before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: Okay, doc, I'm... But if I think about it, this whole... whatever it is, this low mood and feeling guilty all the time and just not caring about my accounts or anything... it's been hanging over me for about four months now. It definitely got a lot harder to ignore after some trouble I had at work. \n\nWhich, man, the wholesale business is just cutthroat right now. Everyone's trying to undercut everyone else. We had this one client, a big regional hardware chain, and I had been buttering up their purchasing manager for like six months. Taking him to lunches, sending him those ridiculous gourmet popcorn tins around the holidays—you know the ones, with the three flavors divided by cardboard? Caramel, cheese, and plain. I don't know who actually eats the plain section, it's basically just packing peanuts. Anyway, I thought I had the account locked down, and then out of nowhere, this guy from a rival firm swoops in and steals it. And get this, the guy who stole it? He used to play minor league baseball. He brings it up in every conversation. \"Oh, that reminds me of when I was playing double-A ball in Toledo.\" Like, nobody cares, Brad! You hit .210 and washed out, you're not Derek Jeter. He even wears his old team windbreaker to trade shows. It's so pathetic. \n\nBut yeah, after that whole mess with the hardware account, that's when this feeling really settled in and I just couldn't shake it anymore. It's been about four months of just... feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least two weeks in the past where you felt down or lost interest in things, even if it wasn't as bad as this time?\n\nPatient: No, really, like I said, nothing lasting two weeks or anything like that before this recent stretch. I mean, sure, I've had a bad weekend here and there, especially if my team loses or something, but I always bounced back by Monday. \n\nI actually used to look forward to Mondays, believe it or not. I had this whole routine. I'd stop at this little coffee shop near the office—not one of the big chains, a local place called \"The Daily Grind.\" Kind of a cliché name, I know. But they had this amazing cold brew. They steeped it for like 24 hours, so it was super smooth, not bitter at all. And the barista, her name was Chloe, she always remembered my order. She had this cool streak of blue in her hair that she'd change up sometimes. One week it was purple, another week it was pink. I asked her about it once, and she said she used this special vegan dye that washes out after a few weeks so she doesn't ruin her hair. I don't know much about hair dye, but it seemed like a lot of upkeep. I can barely remember to get a haircut every six weeks. My barber, Tony, he's always giving me a hard time about it. He's this old Italian guy who's been cutting hair in the same spot since the eighties. He still uses those warm shaving cream dispensers for the back of your neck. It feels incredible, but he talks your ear off about local politics the whole time. \n\nAnyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, being down. No, I've never had anything drag on for weeks like this before. It's totally new for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other periods in your life where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than just a few days, even if it wasn't as bad as what you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it hasn't ever been this bad before, but... yeah, lately I just don't care about anything I used to like doing. I used to play soccer on the weekends, cook dinner for my girlfriend, go check out these small art galleries downtown... I'd even stick around the office to help out the newer sales guys. But now I just can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nWhich is a shame about the cooking, really. My girlfriend loved when I'd make my famous chicken parmesan. The secret is to double-bread the chicken, you know? Flour, egg, breadcrumbs, then back in the egg and breadcrumbs again. It makes this crazy thick crust that holds up to the sauce. I use San Marzano tomatoes for the sauce—they're expensive, like five bucks a can, but totally worth it. The regular ones just taste like tin. I actually went to this Italian grocery store across town to get them, and the parking lot there is a nightmare. The spaces are so narrow, I swear they were painted for golf carts. I saw this lady in a massive SUV try to wedge into a spot and she totally scraped the side of a parked Honda. She just drove off! I couldn't believe it. I tried to get her license plate, but she sped out of there so fast. I ended up leaving a note on the Honda's windshield, just saying what happened. I hope they saw it before it rained, because we had that huge thunderstorm later that afternoon. The thunder was so loud it shook my apartment windows. My neighbor's dog wouldn't stop barking for like three hours. \n\nBut yeah, I just... I haven't cooked or played soccer or done any of that stuff in months. I just don't have the interest anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this one where you felt down or lost interest in things for more than just a few days?\n\nPatient: I feel like we keep circling back to this, doc, but no, I really haven't. Before these last four months, I was always the guy who bounced right back. \n\nI mean, sure, I've been bummed out for a day or two here and there. Like when I failed my driver's test back when I was 16. I was devastated for maybe... a weekend? It was completely rigged, though. The instructor had this massive walrus mustache that entirely covered his mouth. You couldn't even see his lips move when he talked, so half the time I didn't even know he was giving me a direction until he started yelling. And he made me parallel park on this massive hill over on 4th street. Have you ever tried parking a 1998 Ford Taurus on a steep incline? The car was an absolute boat. My dad bought it at a police auction for like eight hundred bucks. It still had the spotlight attached to the driver's side door, which I thought was the coolest thing in the world at the time, but the suspension was completely shot. Every time you hit a pothole, the whole chassis would just rattle. \n\nI actually ended up selling it to this guy on Craigslist a few years later who said he was going to enter it in a demolition derby at the county fair. I always wondered how it did. Probably got crushed in the first round, honestly, the frame was half rust by the time I handed over the keys. The guy paid me entirely in twenty dollar bills, which was kind of sketchy, but hey, cash is cash, right? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I'd be upset about stuff like the driver's test for a couple days, but never anything that just dragged on and on for weeks like what's happening to me now. This is completely new territory.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period in your life before this current one that lasted more than a few days where you felt down or lost interest in things?\n\nPatient: Doc, are we stuck in a time loop or something? Wait, are you asking about *right now*? Because before, you kept asking about *before* this period. Okay, yeah, if you're asking about *this* current period, the one happening right now, then yes. 

I don't know what's wrong with me lately. I just... I don't care about anything. I used to play in this weekend soccer league, you know? Just a casual thing, but I loved it. We had these terrible neon green jerseys that my buddy Steve ordered online. Steve is always trying to find a bargain, but he never checks the sizing charts, so half the team looked like they were wearing compression shirts and the other half looked like they were wearing tents. I think he got them from some sketchy website based out of, I don't know, maybe Eastern Europe? Shipping took like six weeks. The tracking number never even updated. 

But anyway, yeah, I haven't been to a game in weeks. I can't even make myself care. And my girlfriend, I used to cook for her all the time. I make this really good chicken piccata, with the capers and the lemon butter sauce. The trick is you have to pound the chicken cutlets really thin, like, almost paper-thin, otherwise they don't cook evenly and the breading gets soggy. I use a cast iron skillet that I seasoned myself. It took me three days to get the seasoning right, rubbing it with flaxseed oil and baking it upside down in the oven. It smoked up the whole apartment, the fire alarm went off twice. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, she came knocking on the door with her little Pomeranian, thinking the building was on fire. That dog never stops yapping. 

But I haven't cooked anything in... I don't know how long. We just order takeout, or she makes something. I also used to like checking out those small art galleries downtown on First Friday, and at work, I'd usually help out the newer sales guys, show them the ropes with the CRM software because it's a nightmare to navigate. But lately? Nothing. I just can't bring myself to care about any of it. It's like someone just unplugged me.
